Distance

a general stabilizer code has no X and Z sides: d is the minimum Pauli weight of a nontrivial logical operator (a Y factor counts one qubit), and the witness is one Pauli operator that commutes with every generator and is not a product of them
d 36 · witness Pauli weight 36 (4 Y factors; Hamming weight over 2n bits 40) (claimed upper_bound)

Construction & provenance

authors @jacksongquera
provenance submitted through the challenge
novelty novelty not audited
construction Symplectic halving (mirror fold) of the board's CSS entry codes/960-24-40.json ([[960,24,40]], by @MicheldeMessieres), a weight-6 two-block group-algebra (2BGA) code over C24 x Dic5 (construction of arXiv:2306.16400, Lin and Pryadko). The fold uses a fixed-point-free involution tau of the parent's qubits that maps the X-check rowspace onto the Z-check rowspace: tau(1,g) = (2, h * psi(g)) with h = z3x2y1 and psi the automorphism z -> z5, x -> x4, y -> z12x1y3; tau(2, h * psi(g)) = (1, g) (elements indexed as in the parent); block 1 = parent qubits 0..479, block 2 = 480..959. Fold qubit i is the pair {q_i, tau(q_i)}, q_i the i-th smallest qubit with q_i < tau(q_i). Each parent X-check row gives one generator: X on the pairs whose q_i is in the row, Z on the pairs whose tau(q_i) is in the row. S = (A|B) is isotropic, k = 24/2 = 12, check weight 6, not CSS under any local Clifford.
model Claude Opus 5.5 (Claude Code) (claimed, not verified)
builds on arXiv:2306.16400, arXiv:2609.30069, arXiv:2603.05496
date 2026-10-21
notes Derived from codes/960-24-40.json by @MicheldeMessieres (a weight-6 two-block group-algebra (2BGA) code over C24 x Dic5 (construction of arXiv:2306.16400, Lin and Pryadko)). The halving/doubling correspondence is the board's fieldnote fieldnotes/2026-09-28-symplectic-doubling-and-halving.md (@MathysRennela; Prop. 4 of arXiv:2609.30069) and the abelian mirror-code construction of Khesin and Lu (arXiv:2603.05496); the fold convention and the mirror shape are those of @MicheldeMessieres's codes/452-115-18.json and codes/336-10-28.json. Our part, stated narrowly: we enumerated the block-swapping mirrors tau(1,g) = (2, h psi(g)^(+-1)) over every automorphism psi of the group and every h, grouped the folds by the verifier's WL signature, and searched each class. Instruments and depths (lightest Pauli weight found): central-orbit, rung 1, seed 51, 7 partitions x 4000 trials: 36; central-translation pool, rung 1, seed 51, 20 calls x 4000 trials: 65; parent-quotient pool, rung 1, seed 51, 240 calls x 4000 trials: 36; central-orbit, rung 2, seed 52, 7 partitions x 100000 trials: 36; parent-quotient pool, rung 2, seed 52, 540 calls x 20000 trials: 36; ci-plain-doubling, rung ci, seed 52, 2000000 trials: 42. How this code was found

the research note submitted with this code · raw markdown · all notes

[[480,12,36]] — mirror fold of the board's [[960,24,40]]

Direction & hypothesis

Target: the unrestricted weight-6 stabilizer cell. Applying an X/Z-swapping mirror to a strong CSS parent can halve its physical and logical qubits while retaining enough distance to extend this cell's frontier. At submission preparation on 21 October 2026, the best existing kd^2/n in this cell is 23.33, from codes/336-10-28.json. The submitted witness-backed bound gives 32.40. The candidate is non-dominated and dominates no existing entry; the score comparison is separate from Pareto membership.

Parent: codes/960-24-40.json by @MicheldeMessieres, a weight-6 two-block group-algebra (2BGA) code over C24 x Dic5. The 2BGA construction is due to Lin and Pryadko (arXiv:2306.16400).

Fold technique: the mirror-code construction of Khesin and Lu (arXiv:2603.05496); the board's fieldnote fieldnotes/2026-09-28-symplectic-doubling-and-halving.md by @MathysRennela; and the fold convention used by @MicheldeMessieres in codes/336-10-28.json and codes/452-115-18.json, merged in PRs #3079 and #2991. Proposition 4 of arXiv:2609.30069 gives a halving criterion for the CPM family; this 2BGA fold is justified by its explicit mirror and isotropy checks, rather than an assertion that it satisfies that CPM-specific proposition.

The contribution here is the mirror enumeration for this parent, selection of a fold, and the distance evidence below. The parent and the folding operation are credited prior work; a new construction is not claimed.

What was searched

  • Enumerated block swaps tau(1,g) = (2, h psi(g)^epsilon), with epsilon = +1 or -1, over every group automorphism psi and every h in G. The reverse block map is the inverse of the forward map. Retained mirrors that exchange the parent's X- and Z-checks, then grouped the folded presentations by the verifier's Weisfeiler-Lehman (WL) signature.
  • The parent gives three WL classes. All three yielded a lightest found Pauli weight of 36; class 0 is submitted. WL classes do not establish inequivalence under arbitrary changes of stabilizer generators or Clifford transformations.
  • Central-orbit search restricts fold logicals to be constant on orbits of central subgroups whose translations commute with tau. Cyclic and two-generator subgroups of order at most 24 produced seven usable partitions for this fold.
  • Parent-quotient search finds parent logicals constant on cosets of central subgroups, lifts them, applies every central translation, and scores the number of mirror pairs touched. The first rung used 20 central subgroups; the second used nine. The ordinary pool uses unrestricted parent searches followed by the same translations. Plain doubling search uses the identity Pauli frame and re-scores its logicals by Pauli weight.

Evidence trail

All weights below are upper bounds from found logical operators. Trial counts are totals for the named instrument, not a common measure of search strength across instruments.

| Method | Trials | Seed | Lightest Pauli weight found | |---|---:|---:|---:| | Central-orbit, seven partitions | 28,000 | 51 | 36 | | Central-orbit, seven partitions | 700,000 | 52 | 36 | | Parent-quotient pool, 240 calls of 4,000 | 960,000 | 51 | 36 | | Parent-quotient pool, 540 calls of 20,000 | 10,800,000 | 52 | 36 | | Plain central-translation pool, 20 calls of 4,000 | 80,000 | 51 | 65 | | Plain search on the symplectic doubling, Pauli re-scoring | 2,000,000 | 52 | 42 |

The saved candidate gate found no lighter logical in 8,000 RIS trials, seed 1141352680, with the verifier's orbit-fold pass. During submission preparation, research/kit/coordination.py rechecked structure, duplicate detection and frontier membership against the current board and reused this saved refutation. A separate run of verify/qldpc_verify.py on the formatted submission passed with a fresh 8,000-trial refutation, seed 1184000466. Neither run found a logical lighter than 36.

Not run locally: CI's 8,000,000-trial plain accelerated pass. The PR's deep gate targets this pass, subject to its wall-clock budget. Its result is still outstanding.

The submitted S has 480 generators, rank 468, and maximum Pauli check weight 6, giving k = 480 - 468 = 12. The verifier accepts its isotropy, connectivity, non-CSS status under local Cliffords, and stored logical witness. That witness has X support on 28 sites and Z support on 12, with four shared Y sites: 24 X-only + 8 Z-only + 4 Y = 36 qubits. Its unfolded parent X-logical has Hamming weight 40.

Claim: d <= 36, a witness-backed upper bound. No lower bound is claimed. The parent's own d <= 40 does not imply a numerical floor for the fold. Failure to find a lighter logical, including the results 65 and 42 from weaker searches, cannot establish exact distance. Selecting mirrors using found upper bounds also leaves a search-depth selection effect.

The current-board gate reports no exact duplicate or matching WL signature, no dominator, no dominated entry, and no distance-only peer requiring a matched-depth audit. These checks concern this board, not equivalence to all published codes.

This was a targeted paper and catalog review, not an exhaustive literature index or a matrix-equivalence audit. A missing match cannot establish that the instance is new.

Dead ends

  • Folds of codes/720-16-36.json descended with search depth from 36 through 33 and 32 to a found weight of 30 for a [[360,8,<=30]] fold, giving kd^2/n = 20.0.
  • A fold of codes/600-16-30.json gave [[300,8,<=26]], kd^2/n = 18.03. These two alternatives did not improve the targeted score of 23.33. That observation alone does not disqualify a Pareto extension; they are outside this single-code submission.
  • The plain central-translation pool stopped at 65 and the 2,000,000-trial plain doubling search at 42, despite a known weight-36 logical. These searches missed the structured logicals reached by the central-orbit and quotient methods, so neither larger value is used as the submitted distance.

Tools

Code search: Claude Opus 5.5 (Claude Code), matching provenance.model, with about 3.6 CPU-hours as recorded in provenance.search_budget. The search used group automorphism enumeration, NumPy, the repository's GF(2) helpers and gf2_fast backend, plus custom search orchestration. The orchestration is described here rather than supplied as a committed script. Verification uses research/kit/coordination.py, verify/validate_candidate.py and verify/qldpc_verify.py; JSON formatting uses verify/check_codes_format.py. Literature review and submission preparation were performed afterward in Codex; the model attribution for the code search is unchanged.

Reproduction

Use the parent's checks in codes/960-24-40.json. Its group is G = C24 x Dic5 with z central of order 24, x^5 = 1, y^4 = 1 and y x y^-1 = x^-1. Index z^c x^i y^j by 20c + 4i + j, for 0 <= c < 24, 0 <= i < 5 and 0 <= j < 4. Multiplication in these coordinates is (c,i,j)(c',i',j') = (c+c' mod 24, i+(-1)^j i' mod 5, j+j' mod 4). Parent qubits 0..479 form block 1; 480..959 form block 2.

Set h = z^3 x^2 y and define psi on generators by z -> z^5, x -> x^4, y -> z^12 x y^3. Then tau(1,g) = (2,h psi(g)) and tau(2,h psi(g)) = (1,g). This fixed-point-free involution maps the 480 parent X-checks onto the Z-checks.

List the representatives q_i with q_i < tau(q_i) in increasing order. Fold qubit i is the pair {q_i,tau(q_i)}. For every parent X-check in its existing row order, put X on i if the check contains q_i and Z on i if it contains tau(q_i); if both occur, put Y. This reconstructs checks.S in codes/480-12-36.json. The resulting binary symplectic matrix S = (A,B) obeys A B^T + B A^T = 0 over GF(2).

Read the stored witness from distance.P.witness in codes/480-12-36.json. An X component at i unfolds to a parent X on q_i; a Z component unfolds to a parent X on tau(q_i). Both components therefore occupy two parent sites but one folded Y site. For the submitted bound, run uv run python verify/qldpc_verify.py codes/480-12-36.json: it checks the witness's commutation with every generator, non-membership in the stabilizer row space, and Pauli weight 36, as well as running its local refutation pass. Rebuilding this code and checking its witness does not reproduce the entire heuristic search trajectory or prove an exact distance.
